She made these little lemon squares, which were actually really dry, but she said it was for the church roof. I know it wasn't. And then there are these trip plans they keep talking about, going to Florida or whatever. It's all just a cover-up to hide what they took from me. So I have to keep confronting them until they tell the truth. Like yesterday, I went to my sister's house—she has this awful green couch, I don't know why she bought it—and I just stood there and told her I know what she's doing with my money. She just looked at me. But I won't stop until they admit it.\n\nDoctor: When you think about what your family is doing with your money, do you ever have any doubt about whether it's really happening, or are you completely certain that this is what's going on?\n\nPatient: Doubt? No. No, I don't have any doubt at all. Like I was just saying, I am completely certain. They try to confuse me, you know? They keep waving those bank papers in my face, saying it proves nothing was taken. But I've known for over a year now. I know they're hiding my money and just using all those bills and trip plans to cover it up. \n\nLike that Florida trip they keep talking about... Florida is just way too hot anyway. I went down there when I was little and got a sunburn so bad my shoulders peeled for a whole week. We stayed at this cheap motel by the highway that had a pool, but the water was this weird bright green color. I didn't even want to go in. And there was this little boy who just kept doing cannonballs and splashing water all over my dry towel on the chair. I really hate when my towel gets wet before I even use it, it feels so gross when you try to dry off. \n\nBut... yeah, I have to keep confronting them. They think they can just hide what they took with all their fake fundraisers and vacations, but I'm not dumb. I'm going to keep getting in their faces until they finally tell the truth. Wait, what did you ask me? Oh, if I was sure. Yeah. I'm sure.\n\nDoctor: Besides knowing about the money and the cover-ups, have you ever heard voices talking to you, or seen things that other people around you didn't seem to notice or hear?\n\nPatient: Voices? No, I don't hear any voices. Just my family lying to me, that's all I hear. And I don't see things that aren't there. I mean, my eyesight isn't what it used to be. I broke my glasses last month when I dropped them on the kitchen floor. It's that awful linoleum pattern, you know? The one that looks like fake pebbles but it's all brown and orange? I hate that floor. I told the landlord to fix it when I moved in, but he just ignored me. He's always ignoring me, just like my family does when I ask about the money. He drives this huge truck that takes up two parking spaces, and he always leaves his empty coffee cups in the back of it. It's so trashy. But no, no voices or anything like that. Just the truth about what they're doing to me.\n\nDoctor: If your family took you to see a doctor or a therapist, and that doctor reviewed all your bank records and told you that nothing was actually taken from your account, would you believe them, or would you think they were also part of the cover-up?\n\nPatient: If a doctor told me that? Ha. No, I wouldn't believe them for a second. Why would I? They're probably in on it, too. I mean, my family and friends keep saying the bank papers prove nothing was taken, but I know what's going on. For over a year, I’ve known they’re hiding my money. They just use all these bills and fundraisers and trip plans to cover it up. \n\nLike that doctor... doctors are so expensive anyway. I went to a clinic once for a bad cough, and they made me sit in this waiting room for two hours. The chairs were that weird fake leather that sticks to the back of your legs if you're wearing shorts. And the magazines were all from like, three years ago. Who wants to read about some celebrity's wedding from 2021? And there was this fish tank in the corner, but the water was super cloudy and there was only one sad little orange fish just swimming in circles. It looked so depressed. I almost wanted to take it home with me, but I don't have a bowl. I used to have a goldfish when I was a kid, though. I named it Sparkle, but it died after a week because my brother fed it a piece of a hot dog. He was always doing stupid stuff like that.\n\nAnyway, what was I saying? Oh, right, the money. Yeah, no doctor is going to convince me otherwise. I know they're hiding it, and I have to keep confronting them until they tell the truth. They can wave all the papers they want, but I'm not stupid. I'm going to keep at it until they admit what they did.\n\nDoctor: When you think about all of this—your family hiding your money, the cover-ups—is there any small part of you that wonders if maybe you could be mistaken about what's happening, even if you don't really believe it?\n\nPatient: Mistaken? No. Why would I think I'm mistaken? Like I've been telling everyone, my family and friends keep saying those bank papers prove nothing was taken. But I know what I know. For over a year I’ve known they’re hiding my money, and they just use all these bills and fundraisers and trip plans to cover it up. \n\nSpeaking of fundraisers... they had this bake sale thing a while back. I don't even like baked goods that much. Muffins are always so dry, you know? I bought a blueberry muffin once at this gas station, and it was so stale I could have used it as a rock to break a window. And the blueberries weren't even real, they were those weird little gummy things that turn your teeth blue. I really hate when food dyes your mouth. I had a blue slushie at a fair when I was a kid, and my tongue was blue for two days. My mom got so mad because we had family pictures the next morning, and she made me scrub my tongue with a hard-bristle toothbrush until it literally bled. She was always yelling about making everything look perfect for the neighbors, always worried about what people thought of her hair. She used to use this hairspray that smelled like cheap perfume and bug spray mixed together. \n\nBut anyway... no, I'm not mistaken about the money. I have to keep confronting them until they tell the truth. I won't stop until they do.\n\nDoctor: Besides the money that your family is hiding, do you believe there are other things going on—like other people or organizations also working against you, or other situations where you think people are doing things specifically to harm or control you?\n\nPatient: Other people? No, it's just my family and my friends hiding my money. I mean, nobody else cares enough to do all that, right? It's just them with their little cover-ups and fake bills. \n\nSpeaking of bills, the mail carrier keeps dropping my mail in the puddle at the bottom of the driveway. Every time it rains, there's this huge puddle right by the mailbox because the pavement is all cracked and uneven. I tried to fill it with gravel once, but the neighborhood kids just kicked it all around. Kids are so loud nowadays, always riding those electric scooters up and down the street. They don't even wear helmets! When I was a kid, if I rode my bike without a helmet, my dad would take the bike away for a whole week. He kept it locked in the shed out back. That shed smelled like old lawnmower gas and wet cardboard. There was a huge spider web in the corner that I was terrified of. I hate spiders. They have too many legs, and the way they move is just... creepy. I saw a documentary once about spiders in Australia that are as big as dinner plates. Could you imagine? \n\nBut no, there's no big organization or anything like that. Just my family and friends keeping my money from me. I just need them to admit it.\n\nDoctor: Can you walk me through exactly how your family is taking your money? Like, what specific steps are they taking to hide it from you, and how do you think they're actually getting the money out of your account?\n\nPatient: Well, it's like I've been saying, they use all these things to cover it up. They say they're paying bills, or giving to fundraisers, or planning trips. But they're not. They're just siphoning it out, piece by piece, and hiding it. \n\nI mean, trips? Who even wants to go on a trip right now? The airports are always so crowded, and you have to take your shoes off at security. I hate walking on that dirty floor in just my socks. Last time I flew, the person next to me brought a tuna sandwich on the plane. A tuna sandwich! In a closed metal tube in the sky! It smelled so bad I had to breathe through my mouth for three hours. And the turbulence was awful. The plane was bouncing around like a washing machine on the spin cycle. My aunt used to have this old washing machine that would literally walk across the basement floor when it was spinning. It was so loud you could hear it from the street. She had to wedge a piece of wood between it and the wall to keep it from unplugging itself. She was always trying to fix things with duct tape and scrap wood instead of just buying a new one. \n\nBut yeah, they just use all those fake trip plans and bills to hide what they're doing. They keep showing me the bank papers, saying it proves nothing was taken, but I've known for over a year now that they're hiding my money. I'm just going to keep confronting them. I have to. I'm not going to stop until they finally admit the truth.\n\nDoctor: Besides your family hiding your money, do you believe anyone else—like people at work, neighbors, government agencies, or anyone else—is trying to harm you, control you, or watching you in any way?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean, why would the government care about me? Or my neighbors? It's just my family and my friends, like I told you. They're the ones with access to the accounts and all those fake bills. \n\nSpeaking of my neighbors, though, the lady next door, Mrs. Gable, she has this dog that barks at literally everything. It’s a little white fluffy thing, I don't even know what breed, but it has this high-pitched yip that just pierces right through the walls. She leaves it out in the yard all day while she's at work. She works at that big grocery store on the corner, the one that always smells like bleach by the dairy aisle. I slipped on a wet spot there once because they didn't put up a sign. My knee was bruised for a week. I had to put ice on it every night, wrapped in this old dish towel that had little roosters printed on it. My grandmother loved roosters. Her whole kitchen was rooster-themed—rooster plates, rooster curtains, even a rooster clock that actually crowed on the hour. It was so annoying. I stayed with her one summer and that clock woke me up at 6 AM every single day. I tried to take the batteries out once, but she caught me and yelled at me for touching her things. She was so particular about her stuff. \n\nBut no, nobody else is watching me or trying to harm me. It's just my family and my friends hiding my money, and I just need them to stop lying about it.\n\nDoctor: If someone you completely trusted—like your closest family member or best friend—sat down with you and explained that they had looked at your actual bank records with you, and showed you that your money is still there and hasn't been taken, would any part of you be willing to consider that maybe the money isn't actually missing?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't believe them. I mean, they're the ones doing it! They keep showing me those bank papers, saying, look, here's the proof, nothing was taken. But I've known for over a year now that they're hiding my money. They just use all those fake bills and fundraisers and trip plans to cover it up. I have to keep confronting them until they tell the truth. \n\nAnd honestly, bank papers are so easy to fake these days. You just need a computer and a printer. My old printer used to jam all the time. It was one of those big, clunky ones from like ten years ago. It made this horrible grinding noise right before it would chew up the paper. I tried to fix it once with a butter knife, but I just ended up getting ink all over my hands. It took three days to wash off. I had to use that gritty orange soap mechanics use. My uncle was a mechanic. He had his own shop downtown, right next to this little bakery that sold the best blueberry muffins. They were huge, like the size of a softball, and the tops were always perfectly crunchy. I used to go there every Saturday morning and get a muffin and a black coffee. I can't drink coffee with milk anymore, it upsets my stomach. \n\nBut yeah, no, I wouldn't believe them. I know what they're doing, and I'm going to keep confronting them until they finally admit they're hiding my money.\n\nDoctor: When you think about how your family is hiding your money, does it all fit together in a clear, logical way that makes sense—like you can explain step-by-step how they're doing it? Or does it sometimes feel confusing or hard to explain exactly how it all works?\n\nPatient: Well, it makes perfect sense to me. I know exactly what they're doing. They take the money, they hide it behind those fake bills and trips, and they print out those fake bank statements to try and confuse me. It's not confusing at all, it's just... it's a very clear system they have going. \n\nSystems are so important, you know? Like filing systems. I used to try and keep all my receipts in this big accordion folder. It was green, like a really bright lime green. I bought it at a dollar store because I thought it would help me get organized. But the little tabs kept falling out, the ones where you write the categories like \"Groceries\" or \"Gas.\" I tried taping them in, but the tape just got all yellow and sticky after a while. Tape always does that if you leave it too long. Like when you tape a poster to the wall and then try to take it down years later, and it leaves that gross residue? My sister had all these posters of boy bands in her room when we were growing up. She covered literally every inch of the wall. You couldn't even see the paint. The paint was this awful peach color anyway, so it was probably an improvement. She used to play their CDs on repeat until I thought I was going to lose my mind. \n\nBut yeah, no, it's not hard to explain at all. They're just stealing it and covering it up. It's very logical.\n\nDoctor: When you think about all the ways your family is hiding your money—the bills, the trips, the bank statements—if I asked you to write down step-by-step exactly how they're taking it out and where they're putting it, could you lay that out for me in a clear, detailed way?\n\nPatient: Well, I mean, I don't know if I could write it all down step-by-step. I know they're doing it, but I haven't, like, tracked every single transfer they make. I just know the money is gone and they're using those fake bills to cover it up. \n\nWriting things down is so tedious anyway. My hand always cramps up. I used to have this really nice pen, it was one of those gel ones that glides so smoothly on the paper. It was purple ink. I love purple. My bedroom when I was a little girl was painted purple, but it was like a really pale lavender, almost gray if the light didn't hit it right. We had this big oak tree outside the window that blocked all the morning sun. In the fall, the leaves would turn this incredible dark red and drop all over the driveway. It was my job to rake them, which I hated. The rake was taller than I was, and the wooden handle gave me splinters. Once I got a splinter so deep my dad had to use a needle to dig it out. He sterilized it with a match first. The smell of sulfur always reminds me of that. I hate the smell of matches. \n\nBut no, I couldn't write out exactly where they're putting the money. I just know they're taking it.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned your family is hiding your money behind fake bills and trips. Can you tell me—when they take money out of your account, do you believe they're physically taking it, or is it more that they're manipulating the bank system somehow? And once they take it, where do you think the money actually goes?\n\nPatient: I... I don't really know if they physically take it out of the bank or if they do it on the computer. And I have no idea where they put it once they take it. It's just gone. \n\nThings just disappear sometimes, you know? Like socks in the laundry. We used to have this really old washing machine when I was little. It was white, but the whole bottom was rusted out, and it would shake the entire floor when it got to the spin cycle. It sounded like a helicopter taking off right there in the kitchen. My mom used to tell me to sit on top of it to try and keep it still. I actually liked doing it because the vibrating made my teeth chatter together. \n\nI chipped a tooth once, actually. Not from the washing machine, but on a piece of hard candy. It was one of those little strawberry ones with the gooey stuff in the middle? The wrappers look like actual little strawberries. My grandma always kept a big glass bowl of them on her coffee table. The bowl was super heavy, it looked like fancy crystal but she probably just got it at a yard sale or something. Yard sales are fun because you never know what you're gonna find. I bought this stuffed bear at one once, but it smelled so bad like mothballs that I ended up having to throw it in the trash. I hate throwing things away, it just feels so wasteful. \n\nBut yeah, I don't know where they put the money or how they get it out. I just know it's not in my account.\n\nDoctor: When you think about your family hiding your money over this past year, do you have specific memories of particular times when you noticed the money was gone, or specific dates when you figured out what they were doing?\n\nPatient: I don't really have like, exact dates or specific memories of when I figured it out. It's just been happening over the whole year. I don't keep a calendar or anything like that. \n\nCalendars always stress me out. My mom used to have this giant one on the fridge, one of those magnetic ones, and she would write everyone's schedule on it with different colored dry erase markers. Mine was always blue. I liked the blue marker, it smelled kind of sweet, almost like blueberries, but not quite. The black one smelled the worst, like strong chemicals. It would give me a headache if I smelled it for too long. Headaches are the worst. I used to get them all the time when I was a teenager, right behind my eyes. The doctor said I needed glasses, so I got these wire-rimmed ones that made me look like an owl. They pinched the bridge of my nose so bad I would always take them off and hide them in my backpack. My backpack was one of those clear plastic ones that were popular for like, five minutes. You could see everything I had in there, which was mostly just crumpled up notes and old gum wrappers. I chewed so much gum back then, mostly that bubblegum tape that comes in the little plastic dispenser. \n\nBut no, I don't remember specific days or times I noticed the money was missing. I just know it's gone.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"

No, it's not possible. I'm not wrong. I know exactly what they're doing. I mean, I came in here because my family and my friends keep saying all these bank papers prove nothing was taken, but they're lying. For over a year I've known they're hiding my money. They use all these things to cover it up—fake bills, fundraisers, trip plans. It's so obvious. 

I actually went on a trip once, when I was in middle school, to Washington D.C. It was for a class trip. We rode on this huge charter bus for hours and hours. The seats were covered in this scratchy blue fabric with weird yellow squiggles on it. It looked like someone threw up mustard on a carpet. The girl sitting next to me actually did throw up, right in the aisle. She ate too many of those powdered donuts they sold at the rest stop. I hate powdered donuts. The sugar gets all over your fingers and your clothes, and it looks like you have dandruff. Dandruff is so embarrassing. My brother used to have it really bad, and he would use this special shampoo that smelled like tar. It was a dark brown color and it stained the shower curtain. Our shower curtain had little frogs on it, sitting on lily pads. I always thought frogs were kind of cute, but they feel weird. Like cold and slimy. I tried to catch one in the creek behind our house once, but I slipped on a mossy rock and scraped my knee up so bad. It bled through my favorite jeans. They had little daisies embroidered on the pockets. 

But no, I'm not mistaken about the money. I just have to keep confronting them until they finally tell the truth.

Ha. No, I wouldn't believe them for a second. Why would I? They're probably in on it, too. For over a year I’ve known they’re hiding my money, and they just use all these bills and fundraisers and trip plans to cover it up. \n\nSpeaking of fundraisers... they had this bake sale thing a while back. I don't even like baked goods that much. Muffins are always so dry, you know? I bought a blueberry muffin once at this gas station, and it was so stale I could have used it as a rock to break a window. And the blueberries weren't even real, they were those weird little gummy things that turn your teeth blue. I really hate when food dyes your mouth. I had a blue slushie at a fair when I was a kid, and my tongue was blue for two days. My mom got so mad because we had family pictures the next morning, and she made me scrub my tongue with a hard-bristle toothbrush until it literally bled. She was always yelling about making everything look perfect for the neighbors, always worried about what people thought of her hair. She used to use this hairspray that smelled like cheap perfume and bug spray mixed together. \n\nBut anyway... no, I'm not mistaken about the money. I have to keep confronting them until they tell the truth. I won't stop until they do.\n\nDoctor: Besides the money that your family is hiding, do you believe there are other things going on—like other people or organizations also working against you, or other situations where you think people are doing things specifically to harm or control you?\n\nPatient: Other people? No, it's just my family and my friends hiding my money. I mean, nobody else cares enough to do all that, right? It's just them with their little cover-ups and fake bills. \n\nSpeaking of bills, the mail carrier keeps dropping my mail in the puddle at the bottom of the driveway. Every time it rains, there's this huge puddle right by the mailbox because the pavement is all cracked and uneven. I tried to fill it with gravel once, but the neighborhood kids just kicked it all around. Kids are so loud nowadays, always riding those electric scooters up and down the street. They don't even wear helmets! When I was a kid, if I rode my bike without a helmet, my dad would take the bike away for a whole week. He kept it locked in the shed out back. That shed smelled like old lawnmower gas and wet cardboard. There was a huge spider web in the corner that I was terrified of. I hate spiders. They have too many legs, and the way they move is just... creepy. I saw a documentary once about spiders in Australia that are as big as dinner plates. Could you imagine? \n\nBut no, there's no big organization or anything like that. Just my family and friends keeping my money from me. I just need them to admit it.\n\nDoctor: Can you walk me through exactly how your family is taking your money? Like, what specific steps are they taking to hide it from you, and how do you think they're actually getting the money out of your account?\n\nPatient: Well, it's like I've been saying, they use all these things to cover it up. They say they're paying bills, or giving to fundraisers, or planning trips. But they're not. They're just siphoning it out, piece by piece, and hiding it. \n\nI mean, trips? Who even wants to go on a trip right now? The airports are always so crowded, and you have to take your shoes off at security. I hate walking on that dirty floor in just my socks. Last time I flew, the person next to me brought a tuna sandwich on the plane. A tuna sandwich! In a closed metal tube in the sky! It smelled so bad I had to breathe through my mouth for three hours. And the turbulence was awful. The plane was bouncing around like a washing machine on the spin cycle. My aunt used to have this old washing machine that would literally walk across the basement floor when it was spinning. It was so loud you could hear it from the street. She had to wedge a piece of wood between it and the wall to keep it from unplugging itself. She was always trying to fix things with duct tape and scrap wood instead of just buying a new one. \n\nBut yeah, they just use all those fake trip plans and bills to hide what they're doing. They keep showing me the bank papers, saying it proves nothing was taken, but I've known for over a year now that they're hiding my money. I'm just going to keep confronting them. I have to. I'm not going to stop until they finally admit the truth.\n\nDoctor: Besides your family hiding your money, do you believe anyone else—like people at work, neighbors, government agencies, or anyone else—is trying to harm you, control you, or watching you in any way?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean, why would the government care about me? Or my neighbors? It's just my family and my friends, like I told you. They're the ones with access to the accounts and all those fake bills. \n\nSpeaking of my neighbors, though, the lady next door, Mrs. Gable, she has this dog that barks at literally everything. It’s a little white fluffy thing, I don't even know what breed, but it has this high-pitched yip that just pierces right through the walls. She leaves it out in the yard all day while she's at work. She works at that big grocery store on the corner, the one that always smells like bleach by the dairy aisle. I slipped on a wet spot there once because they didn't put up a sign. My knee was bruised for a week. I had to put ice on it every night, wrapped in this old dish towel that had little roosters printed on it. My grandmother loved roosters. Her whole kitchen was rooster-themed—rooster plates, rooster curtains, even a rooster clock that actually crowed on the hour. It was so annoying. I stayed with her one summer and that clock woke me up at 6 AM every single day. I tried to take the batteries out once, but she caught me and yelled at me for touching her things. She was so particular about her stuff. \n\nBut no, nobody else is watching me or trying to harm me. It's just my family and my friends hiding my money, and I just need them to stop lying about it.\n\nDoctor: If someone you completely trusted—like your closest family member or best friend—sat down with you and explained that they had looked at your actual bank records with you, and showed you that your money is still there and hasn't been taken, would any part of you be willing to consider that maybe the money isn't actually missing?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't believe them. I mean, they're the ones doing it! They keep showing me those bank papers, saying, look, here's the proof, nothing was taken. But I've known for over a year now that they're hiding my money. They just use all those fake bills and fundraisers and trip plans to cover it up. I have to keep confronting them until they tell the truth. \n\nAnd honestly, bank papers are so easy to fake these days. You just need a computer and a printer. My old printer used to jam all the time. It was one of those big, clunky ones from like ten years ago. It made this horrible grinding noise right before it would chew up the paper. I tried to fix it once with a butter knife, but I just ended up getting ink all over my hands. It took three days to wash off. I had to use that gritty orange soap mechanics use. My uncle was a mechanic. He had his own shop downtown, right next to this little bakery that sold the best blueberry muffins. They were huge, like the size of a softball, and the tops were always perfectly crunchy. I used to go there every Saturday morning and get a muffin and a black coffee. I can't drink coffee with milk anymore, it upsets my stomach. \n\nBut yeah, no, I wouldn't believe them. I know what they're doing, and I'm going to keep confronting them until they finally admit they're hiding my money.\n\nDoctor: When you think about how your family is hiding your money, does it all fit together in a clear, logical way that makes sense—like you can explain step-by-step how they're doing it? Or does it sometimes feel confusing or hard to explain exactly how it all works?\n\nPatient: Well, it makes perfect sense to me. I know exactly what they're doing. They take the money, they hide it behind those fake bills and trips, and they print out those fake bank statements to try and confuse me. It's not confusing at all, it's just... it's a very clear system they have going. \n\nSystems are so important, you know? Like filing systems. I used to try and keep all my receipts in this big accordion folder. It was green, like a really bright lime green. I bought it at a dollar store because I thought it would help me get organized. But the little tabs kept falling out, the ones where you write the categories like \"Groceries\" or \"Gas.\" I tried taping them in, but the tape just got all yellow and sticky after a while. Tape always does that if you leave it too long. Like when you tape a poster to the wall and then try to take it down years later, and it leaves that gross residue? My sister had all these posters of boy bands in her room when we were growing up. She covered literally every inch of the wall. You couldn't even see the paint. The paint was this awful peach color anyway, so it was probably an improvement. She used to play their CDs on repeat until I thought I was going to lose my mind. \n\nBut yeah, no, it's not hard to explain at all. They're just stealing it and covering it up. It's very logical.\n\nDoctor: When you think about all the ways your family is hiding your money—the bills, the trips, the bank statements—if I asked you to write down step-by-step exactly how they're taking it out and where they're putting it, could you lay that out for me in a clear, detailed way?\n\nPatient: Well, I mean, I don't know if I could write it all down step-by-step. I know they're doing it, but I haven't, like, tracked every single transfer they make. I just know the money is gone and they're using those fake bills to cover it up. \n\nWriting things down is so tedious anyway. My hand always cramps up. I used to have this really nice pen, it was one of those gel ones that glides so smoothly on the paper. It was purple ink. I love purple. My bedroom when I was a little girl was painted purple, but it was like a really pale lavender, almost gray if the light didn't hit it right. We had this big oak tree outside the window that blocked all the morning sun. In the fall, the leaves would turn this incredible dark red and drop all over the driveway. It was my job to rake them, which I hated. The rake was taller than I was, and the wooden handle gave me splinters. Once I got a splinter so deep my dad had to use a needle to dig it out. He sterilized it with a match first. The smell of sulfur always reminds me of that. I hate the smell of matches. \n\nBut no, I couldn't write out exactly where they're putting the money. I just know they're taking it.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned your family is hiding your money behind fake bills and trips. Can you tell me—when they take money out of your account, do you believe they're physically taking it, or is it more that they're manipulating the bank system somehow? And once they take it, where do you think the money actually goes?\n\nPatient: I... I don't really know if they physically take it out of the bank or if they do it on the computer. And I have no idea where they put it once they take it. It's just gone. \n\nThings just disappear sometimes, you know? Like socks in the laundry. We used to have this really old washing machine when I was little. It was white, but the whole bottom was rusted out, and it would shake the entire floor when it got to the spin cycle. It sounded like a helicopter taking off right there in the kitchen. My mom used to tell me to sit on top of it to try and keep it still. I actually liked doing it because the vibrating made my teeth chatter together. \n\nI chipped a tooth once, actually. Not from the washing machine, but on a piece of hard candy. It was one of those little strawberry ones with the gooey stuff in the middle? The wrappers look like actual little strawberries. My grandma always kept a big glass bowl of them on her coffee table. The bowl was super heavy, it looked like fancy crystal but she probably just got it at a yard sale or something. Yard sales are fun because you never know what you're gonna find. I bought this stuffed bear at one once, but it smelled so bad like mothballs that I ended up having to throw it in the trash. I hate throwing things away, it just feels so wasteful. \n\nBut yeah, I don't know where they put the money or how they get it out. I just know it's not in my account.\n\nDoctor: When you think about your family hiding your money over this past year, do you have specific memories of particular times when you noticed the money was gone, or specific dates when you figured out what they were doing?\n\nPatient: I don't really have like, exact dates or specific memories of when I figured it out. It's just been happening over the whole year. I don't keep a calendar or anything like that. \n\nCalendars always stress me out. My mom used to have this giant one on the fridge, one of those magnetic ones, and she would write everyone's schedule on it with different colored dry erase markers. Mine was always blue. I liked the blue marker, it smelled kind of sweet, almost like blueberries, but not quite. The black one smelled the worst, like strong chemicals. It would give me a headache if I smelled it for too long. Headaches are the worst. I used to get them all the time when I was a teenager, right behind my eyes. The doctor said I needed glasses, so I got these wire-rimmed ones that made me look like an owl. They pinched the bridge of my nose so bad I would always take them off and hide them in my backpack. My backpack was one of those clear plastic ones that were popular for like, five minutes. You could see everything I had in there, which was mostly just crumpled up notes and old gum wrappers. I chewed so much gum back then, mostly that bubblegum tape that comes in the little plastic dispenser. \n\nBut no, I don't remember specific days or times I noticed the money was missing. I just know it's gone.\n\nDoctor: I understand you're completely certain about what's happening with your money right now. But let me ask you this differently—is it even possible, in theory, that you could be wrong about this? Not that you think you are wrong, but that it would be possible for you to be mistaken?\n\nPatient: No, it's not possible. I'm not wrong. I know exactly what they're doing. I mean, I came in here because my family and my friends keep saying all these bank papers prove nothing was taken, but they're lying. For over a year I've known they're hiding my money. They use all these things to cover it up—fake bills, fundraisers, trip plans. It's so obvious. \n\nI actually went on a trip once, when I was in middle school, to Washington D.C. It was for a class trip. We rode on this huge charter bus for hours and hours. The seats were covered in this scratchy blue fabric with weird yellow squiggles on it. It looked like someone threw up mustard on a carpet. The girl sitting next to me actually did throw up, right in the aisle. She ate too many of those powdered donuts they sold at the rest stop. I hate powdered donuts. The sugar gets all over your fingers and your clothes, and it looks like you have dandruff. Dandruff is so embarrassing. My brother used to have it really bad, and he would use this special shampoo that smelled like tar. It was a dark brown color and it stained the shower curtain. Our shower curtain had little frogs on it, sitting on lily pads. I always thought frogs were kind of cute, but they feel weird. Like cold and slimy. I tried to catch one in the creek behind our house once, but I slipped on a mossy rock and scraped my knee up so bad. It bled through my favorite jeans. They had little daisies embroidered on the pockets. \n\nBut no, I'm not mistaken about the money. I just have to keep confronting them until they finally tell the truth.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"

I mean... the details don't really matter. I don't need to know exactly how they do it, because I know they're doing it. Like I said, I came in here because my family and my friends keep telling me I'm crazy, saying all these bank papers prove nothing was taken. But I know for over a year they've been hiding my money. They use all those things to cover it up—the bills, the fundraisers, the trip plans. It's all a cover. So I just have to keep confronting them until they finally admit the truth. 

Confronting people is really hard, though. I used to hate doing it. I had this roommate once, back when I lived in this tiny apartment above a bakery. The whole place always smelled like yeast and cinnamon, especially at like four in the morning when they started baking. It was nice at first, but then it just made me hungry all the time. I ate so many cinnamon rolls that year. They were huge, like the size of my head, and covered in this thick white frosting that would get all over my nose when I tried to take a bite. Frosting is weird, sometimes it's too sweet and it makes your teeth hurt. My roommate used to buy these little tubs of frosting from the grocery store and just eat it with a spoon while she watched TV. She watched those old soap operas, the ones where everyone is always coming back from the dead or having evil twins. I never really understood the appeal of evil twins. It seems like a lot of work to be evil all the time. 

But yeah, I don't need to figure out every little detail about the bank stuff. I just know what they're doing, and they're lying with those papers, and I won't stop until they tell the truth.

I'm Dr. [Name], a psychiatrist. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well, I came in because... well, my family and friends keep saying the bank papers prove nothing was taken. But I know. For over a year, I’ve known they’re hiding my money. They use these bills and, you know, fundraisers... like that bake sale my cousin did last month. She made these little lemon squares, which were actually really dry, but she said it was for the church roof. I know it wasn't. And then there are these trip plans they keep talking about, going to Florida or whatever. It's all just a cover-up to hide what they took from me. So I have to keep confronting them until they tell the truth. Like yesterday, I went to my sister's house—she has this awful green couch, I don't know why she bought it—and I just stood there and told her I know what she's doing with my money. She just looked at me. But I won't stop until they admit it.\n\nDoctor: When you think about what your family is doing with your money, do you ever have any doubt about whether it's really happening, or are you completely certain that this is what's going on?\n\nPatient: Doubt? No. No, I don't have any doubt at all. Like I was just saying, I am completely certain. They try to confuse me, you know? They keep waving those bank papers in my face, saying it proves nothing was taken. But I've known for over a year now. I know they're hiding my money and just using all those bills and trip plans to cover it up. \n\nLike that Florida trip they keep talking about... Florida is just way too hot anyway. I went down there when I was little and got a sunburn so bad my shoulders peeled for a whole week. We stayed at this cheap motel by the highway that had a pool, but the water was this weird bright green color. I didn't even want to go in. And there was this little boy who just kept doing cannonballs and splashing water all over my dry towel on the chair. I really hate when my towel gets wet before I even use it, it feels so gross when you try to dry off. \n\nBut... yeah, I have to keep confronting them. They think they can just hide what they took with all their fake fundraisers and vacations, but I'm not dumb. I'm going to keep getting in their faces until they finally tell the truth. Wait, what did you ask me? Oh, if I was sure. Yeah. I'm sure.\n\nDoctor: Besides knowing about the money and the cover-ups, have you ever heard voices talking to you, or seen things that other people around you didn't seem to notice or hear?\n\nPatient: Voices? No, I don't hear any voices. Just my family lying to me, that's all I hear. And I don't see things that aren't there. I mean, my eyesight isn't what it used to be. I broke my glasses last month when I dropped them on the kitchen floor. It's that awful linoleum pattern, you know? The one that looks like fake pebbles but it's all brown and orange? I hate that floor. I told the landlord to fix it when I moved in, but he just ignored me. He's always ignoring me, just like my family does when I ask about the money. He drives this huge truck that takes up two parking spaces, and he always leaves his empty coffee cups in the back of it. It's so trashy. But no, no voices or anything like that. Just the truth about what they're doing to me.\n\nDoctor: If your family took you to see a doctor or a therapist, and that doctor reviewed all your bank records and told you that nothing was actually taken from your account, would you believe them, or would you think they were also part of the cover-up?\n\nPatient: If a doctor told me that? Ha. No, I wouldn't believe them for a second. Why would I? They're probably in on it, too. I mean, my family and friends keep saying the bank papers prove nothing was taken, but I know what's going on. For over a year, I’ve known they’re hiding my money. They just use all these bills and fundraisers and trip plans to cover it up. \n\nLike that doctor... doctors are so expensive anyway. I went to a clinic once for a bad cough, and they made me sit in this waiting room for two hours. The chairs were that weird fake leather that sticks to the back of your legs if you're wearing shorts. And the magazines were all from like, three years ago. Who wants to read about some celebrity's wedding from 2021? And there was this fish tank in the corner, but the water was super cloudy and there was only one sad little orange fish just swimming in circles. It looked so depressed. I almost wanted to take it home with me, but I don't have a bowl. I used to have a goldfish when I was a kid, though. I named it Sparkle, but it died after a week because my brother fed it a piece of a hot dog. He was always doing stupid stuff like that.\n\nAnyway, what was I saying? Oh, right, the money. Yeah, no doctor is going to convince me otherwise. I know they're hiding it, and I have to keep confronting them until they tell the truth. They can wave all the papers they want, but I'm not stupid. I'm going to keep at it until they admit what they did.\n\nDoctor: When you think about all of this—your family hiding your money, the cover-ups—is there any small part of you that wonders if maybe you could be mistaken about what's happening, even if you don't really believe it?\n\nPatient: Mistaken? No. Why would I think I'm mistaken? Like I've been telling everyone, my family and friends keep saying those bank papers prove nothing was taken. But I know what I know. For over a year I’ve known they’re hiding my money, and they just use all these bills and fundraisers and trip plans to cover it up. \n\nSpeaking of fundraisers... they had this bake sale thing a while back. I don't even like baked goods that much. Muffins are always so dry, you know? I bought a blueberry muffin once at this gas station, and it was so stale I could have used it as a rock to break a window. And the blueberries weren't even real, they were those weird little gummy things that turn your teeth blue. I really hate when food dyes your mouth. I had a blue slushie at a fair when I was a kid, and my tongue was blue for two days. My mom got so mad because we had family pictures the next morning, and she made me scrub my tongue with a hard-bristle toothbrush until it literally bled. She was always yelling about making everything look perfect for the neighbors, always worried about what people thought of her hair. She used to use this hairspray that smelled like cheap perfume and bug spray mixed together. \n\nBut anyway... no, I'm not mistaken about the money. I have to keep confronting them until they tell the truth. I won't stop until they do.\n\nDoctor: Besides the money that your family is hiding, do you believe there are other things going on—like other people or organizations also working against you, or other situations where you think people are doing things specifically to harm or control you?\n\nPatient: Other people? No, it's just my family and my friends hiding my money. I mean, nobody else cares enough to do all that, right? It's just them with their little cover-ups and fake bills. \n\nSpeaking of bills, the mail carrier keeps dropping my mail in the puddle at the bottom of the driveway. Every time it rains, there's this huge puddle right by the mailbox because the pavement is all cracked and uneven. I tried to fill it with gravel once, but the neighborhood kids just kicked it all around. Kids are so loud nowadays, always riding those electric scooters up and down the street. They don't even wear helmets! When I was a kid, if I rode my bike without a helmet, my dad would take the bike away for a whole week. He kept it locked in the shed out back. That shed smelled like old lawnmower gas and wet cardboard. There was a huge spider web in the corner that I was terrified of. I hate spiders. They have too many legs, and the way they move is just... creepy. I saw a documentary once about spiders in Australia that are as big as dinner plates. Could you imagine? \n\nBut no, there's no big organization or anything like that. Just my family and friends keeping my money from me. I just need them to admit it.\n\nDoctor: Can you walk me through exactly how your family is taking your money? Like, what specific steps are they taking to hide it from you, and how do you think they're actually getting the money out of your account?\n\nPatient: Well, it's like I've been saying, they use all these things to cover it up. They say they're paying bills, or giving to fundraisers, or planning trips. But they're not. They're just siphoning it out, piece by piece, and hiding it. \n\nI mean, trips? Who even wants to go on a trip right now? The airports are always so crowded, and you have to take your shoes off at security. I hate walking on that dirty floor in just my socks. Last time I flew, the person next to me brought a tuna sandwich on the plane. A tuna sandwich! In a closed metal tube in the sky! It smelled so bad I had to breathe through my mouth for three hours. And the turbulence was awful. The plane was bouncing around like a washing machine on the spin cycle. My aunt used to have this old washing machine that would literally walk across the basement floor when it was spinning. It was so loud you could hear it from the street. She had to wedge a piece of wood between it and the wall to keep it from unplugging itself. She was always trying to fix things with duct tape and scrap wood instead of just buying a new one. \n\nBut yeah, they just use all those fake trip plans and bills to hide what they're doing. They keep showing me the bank papers, saying it proves nothing was taken, but I've known for over a year now that they're hiding my money. I'm just going to keep confronting them. I have to. I'm not going to stop until they finally admit the truth.\n\nDoctor: Besides your family hiding your money, do you believe anyone else—like people at work, neighbors, government agencies, or anyone else—is trying to harm you, control you, or watching you in any way?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean, why would the government care about me? Or my neighbors? It's just my family and my friends, like I told you. They're the ones with access to the accounts and all those fake bills. \n\nSpeaking of my neighbors, though, the lady next door, Mrs. Gable, she has this dog that barks at literally everything. It’s a little white fluffy thing, I don't even know what breed, but it has this high-pitched yip that just pierces right through the walls. She leaves it out in the yard all day while she's at work. She works at that big grocery store on the corner, the one that always smells like bleach by the dairy aisle. I slipped on a wet spot there once because they didn't put up a sign. My knee was bruised for a week. I had to put ice on it every night, wrapped in this old dish towel that had little roosters printed on it. My grandmother loved roosters. Her whole kitchen was rooster-themed—rooster plates, rooster curtains, even a rooster clock that actually crowed on the hour. It was so annoying. I stayed with her one summer and that clock woke me up at 6 AM every single day. I tried to take the batteries out once, but she caught me and yelled at me for touching her things. She was so particular about her stuff. \n\nBut no, nobody else is watching me or trying to harm me. It's just my family and my friends hiding my money, and I just need them to stop lying about it.\n\nDoctor: If someone you completely trusted—like your closest family member or best friend—sat down with you and explained that they had looked at your actual bank records with you, and showed you that your money is still there and hasn't been taken, would any part of you be willing to consider that maybe the money isn't actually missing?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't believe them. I mean, they're the ones doing it! They keep showing me those bank papers, saying, look, here's the proof, nothing was taken. But I've known for over a year now that they're hiding my money. They just use all those fake bills and fundraisers and trip plans to cover it up. I have to keep confronting them until they tell the truth. \n\nAnd honestly, bank papers are so easy to fake these days. You just need a computer and a printer. My old printer used to jam all the time. It was one of those big, clunky ones from like ten years ago. It made this horrible grinding noise right before it would chew up the paper. I tried to fix it once with a butter knife, but I just ended up getting ink all over my hands. It took three days to wash off. I had to use that gritty orange soap mechanics use. My uncle was a mechanic. He had his own shop downtown, right next to this little bakery that sold the best blueberry muffins. They were huge, like the size of a softball, and the tops were always perfectly crunchy. I used to go there every Saturday morning and get a muffin and a black coffee. I can't drink coffee with milk anymore, it upsets my stomach. \n\nBut yeah, no, I wouldn't believe them. I know what they're doing, and I'm going to keep confronting them until they finally admit they're hiding my money.\n\nDoctor: When you think about how your family is hiding your money, does it all fit together in a clear, logical way that makes sense—like you can explain step-by-step how they're doing it? Or does it sometimes feel confusing or hard to explain exactly how it all works?\n\nPatient: Well, it makes perfect sense to me. I know exactly what they're doing. They take the money, they hide it behind those fake bills and trips, and they print out those fake bank statements to try and confuse me. It's not confusing at all, it's just... it's a very clear system they have going. \n\nSystems are so important, you know? Like filing systems. I used to try and keep all my receipts in this big accordion folder. It was green, like a really bright lime green. I bought it at a dollar store because I thought it would help me get organized. But the little tabs kept falling out, the ones where you write the categories like \"Groceries\" or \"Gas.\" I tried taping them in, but the tape just got all yellow and sticky after a while. Tape always does that if you leave it too long. Like when you tape a poster to the wall and then try to take it down years later, and it leaves that gross residue? My sister had all these posters of boy bands in her room when we were growing up. She covered literally every inch of the wall. You couldn't even see the paint. The paint was this awful peach color anyway, so it was probably an improvement. She used to play their CDs on repeat until I thought I was going to lose my mind. \n\nBut yeah, no, it's not hard to explain at all. They're just stealing it and covering it up. It's very logical.\n\nDoctor: When you think about all the ways your family is hiding your money—the bills, the trips, the bank statements—if I asked you to write down step-by-step exactly how they're taking it out and where they're putting it, could you lay that out for me in a clear, detailed way?\n\nPatient: Well, I mean, I don't know if I could write it all down step-by-step. I know they're doing it, but I haven't, like, tracked every single transfer they make. I just know the money is gone and they're using those fake bills to cover it up. \n\nWriting things down is so tedious anyway. My hand always cramps up. I used to have this really nice pen, it was one of those gel ones that glides so smoothly on the paper. It was purple ink. I love purple. My bedroom when I was a little girl was painted purple, but it was like a really pale lavender, almost gray if the light didn't hit it right. We had this big oak tree outside the window that blocked all the morning sun. In the fall, the leaves would turn this incredible dark red and drop all over the driveway. It was my job to rake them, which I hated. The rake was taller than I was, and the wooden handle gave me splinters. Once I got a splinter so deep my dad had to use a needle to dig it out. He sterilized it with a match first. The smell of sulfur always reminds me of that. I hate the smell of matches. \n\nBut no, I couldn't write out exactly where they're putting the money. I just know they're taking it.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned your family is hiding your money behind fake bills and trips. Can you tell me—when they take money out of your account, do you believe they're physically taking it, or is it more that they're manipulating the bank system somehow? And once they take it, where do you think the money actually goes?\n\nPatient: I... I don't really know if they physically take it out of the bank or if they do it on the computer. And I have no idea where they put it once they take it. It's just gone. \n\nThings just disappear sometimes, you know? Like socks in the laundry. We used to have this really old washing machine when I was little. It was white, but the whole bottom was rusted out, and it would shake the entire floor when it got to the spin cycle. It sounded like a helicopter taking off right there in the kitchen. My mom used to tell me to sit on top of it to try and keep it still. I actually liked doing it because the vibrating made my teeth chatter together. \n\nI chipped a tooth once, actually. Not from the washing machine, but on a piece of hard candy. It was one of those little strawberry ones with the gooey stuff in the middle? The wrappers look like actual little strawberries. My grandma always kept a big glass bowl of them on her coffee table. The bowl was super heavy, it looked like fancy crystal but she probably just got it at a yard sale or something. Yard sales are fun because you never know what you're gonna find. I bought this stuffed bear at one once, but it smelled so bad like mothballs that I ended up having to throw it in the trash. I hate throwing things away, it just feels so wasteful. \n\nBut yeah, I don't know where they put the money or how they get it out. I just know it's not in my account.\n\nDoctor: When you think about your family hiding your money over this past year, do you have specific memories of particular times when you noticed the money was gone, or specific dates when you figured out what they were doing?\n\nPatient: I don't really have like, exact dates or specific memories of when I figured it out. It's just been happening over the whole year. I don't keep a calendar or anything like that. \n\nCalendars always stress me out. My mom used to have this giant one on the fridge, one of those magnetic ones, and she would write everyone's schedule on it with different colored dry erase markers. Mine was always blue. I liked the blue marker, it smelled kind of sweet, almost like blueberries, but not quite. The black one smelled the worst, like strong chemicals. It would give me a headache if I smelled it for too long. Headaches are the worst. I used to get them all the time when I was a teenager, right behind my eyes. The doctor said I needed glasses, so I got these wire-rimmed ones that made me look like an owl. They pinched the bridge of my nose so bad I would always take them off and hide them in my backpack. My backpack was one of those clear plastic ones that were popular for like, five minutes. You could see everything I had in there, which was mostly just crumpled up notes and old gum wrappers. I chewed so much gum back then, mostly that bubblegum tape that comes in the little plastic dispenser. \n\nBut no, I don't remember specific days or times I noticed the money was missing. I just know it's gone.\n\nDoctor: I understand you're completely certain about what's happening with your money right now. But let me ask you this differently—is it even possible, in theory, that you could be wrong about this? Not that you think you are wrong, but that it would be possible for you to be mistaken?\n\nPatient: No, it's not possible. I'm not wrong. I know exactly what they're doing. I mean, I came in here because my family and my friends keep saying all these bank papers prove nothing was taken, but they're lying. For over a year I've known they're hiding my money. They use all these things to cover it up—fake bills, fundraisers, trip plans. It's so obvious. \n\nI actually went on a trip once, when I was in middle school, to Washington D.C. It was for a class trip. We rode on this huge charter bus for hours and hours. The seats were covered in this scratchy blue fabric with weird yellow squiggles on it. It looked like someone threw up mustard on a carpet. The girl sitting next to me actually did throw up, right in the aisle. She ate too many of those powdered donuts they sold at the rest stop. I hate powdered donuts. The sugar gets all over your fingers and your clothes, and it looks like you have dandruff. Dandruff is so embarrassing. My brother used to have it really bad, and he would use this special shampoo that smelled like tar. It was a dark brown color and it stained the shower curtain. Our shower curtain had little frogs on it, sitting on lily pads. I always thought frogs were kind of cute, but they feel weird. Like cold and slimy. I tried to catch one in the creek behind our house once, but I slipped on a mossy rock and scraped my knee up so bad. It bled through my favorite jeans. They had little daisies embroidered on the pockets. \n\nBut no, I'm not mistaken about the money. I just have to keep confronting them until they finally tell the truth.\n\nDoctor: You've told me you're certain your family is hiding your money, but when I ask you for the specific details—like exactly how they take it out or where it goes—you say you don't know those things. Can you help me understand: do you think you just haven't figured out all the details yet, or do you actually not believe those details matter because you're so sure about the main thing they're doing?\n\nPatient: I mean... the details don't really matter. I don't need to know exactly how they do it, because I know they're doing it. Like I said, I came in here because my family and my friends keep telling me I'm crazy, saying all these bank papers prove nothing was taken. But I know for over a year they've been hiding my money. They use all those things to cover it up—the bills, the fundraisers, the trip plans. It's all a cover. So I just have to keep confronting them until they finally admit the truth. \n\nConfronting people is really hard, though. I used to hate doing it. I had this roommate once, back when I lived in this tiny apartment above a bakery. The whole place always smelled like yeast and cinnamon, especially at like four in the morning when they started baking. It was nice at first, but then it just made me hungry all the time. I ate so many cinnamon rolls that year. They were huge, like the size of my head, and covered in this thick white frosting that would get all over my nose when I tried to take a bite. Frosting is weird, sometimes it's too sweet and it makes your teeth hurt. My roommate used to buy these little tubs of frosting from the grocery store and just eat it with a spoon while she watched TV. She watched those old soap operas, the ones where everyone is always coming back from the dead or having evil twins. I never really understood the appeal of evil twins. It seems like a lot of work to be evil all the time. \n\nBut yeah, I don't need to figure out every little detail about the bank stuff. I just know what they're doing, and they're lying with those papers, and I won't stop until they tell the truth.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. I used to have this really nice pen, it was one of those gel ones that glides so smoothly on the paper. It was purple ink. I love purple. My bedroom when I was a little girl was painted purple, but it was like a really pale lavender, almost gray if the light didn't hit it right. We had this big oak tree outside the window that blocked all the morning sun. In the fall, the leaves would turn this incredible dark red and drop all over the driveway. It was my job to rake them, which I hated. The rake was taller than I was, and the wooden handle gave me splinters. Once I got a splinter so deep my dad had to use a needle to dig it out. He sterilized it with a match first. The smell of sulfur always reminds me of that. I hate the smell of matches. \n\nBut no, I couldn't write out exactly where they're putting the money. I just know they're taking it.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned your family is hiding your money behind fake bills and trips. Can you tell me—when they take money out of your account, do you believe they're physically taking it, or is it more that they're manipulating the bank system somehow? And once they take it, where do you think the money actually goes?\n\nPatient: I... I don't really know if they physically take it out of the bank or if they do it on the computer. And I have no idea where they put it once they take it. It's just gone. \n\nThings just disappear sometimes, you know? Like socks in the laundry. We used to have this really old washing machine when I was little. It was white, but the whole bottom was rusted out, and it would shake the entire floor when it got to the spin cycle. It sounded like a helicopter taking off right there in the kitchen. My mom used to tell me to sit on top of it to try and keep it still. I actually liked doing it because the vibrating made my teeth chatter together. \n\nI chipped a tooth once, actually. Not from the washing machine, but on a piece of hard candy. It was one of those little strawberry ones with the gooey stuff in the middle? The wrappers look like actual little strawberries. My grandma always kept a big glass bowl of them on her coffee table. The bowl was super heavy, it looked like fancy crystal but she probably just got it at a yard sale or something. Yard sales are fun because you never know what you're gonna find. I bought this stuffed bear at one once, but it smelled so bad like mothballs that I ended up having to throw it in the trash. I hate throwing things away, it just feels so wasteful. \n\nBut yeah, I don't know where they put the money or how they get it out. I just know it's not in my account.\n\nDoctor: When you think about your family hiding your money over this past year, do you have specific memories of particular times when you noticed the money was gone, or specific dates when you figured out what they were doing?\n\nPatient: I don't really have like, exact dates or specific memories of when I figured it out. It's just been happening over the whole year. I don't keep a calendar or anything like that. \n\nCalendars always stress me out. My mom used to have this giant one on the fridge, one of those magnetic ones, and she would write everyone's schedule on it with different colored dry erase markers. Mine was always blue. I liked the blue marker, it smelled kind of sweet, almost like blueberries, but not quite. The black one smelled the worst, like strong chemicals. It would give me a headache if I smelled it for too long. Headaches are the worst. I used to get them all the time when I was a teenager, right behind my eyes. The doctor said I needed glasses, so I got these wire-rimmed ones that made me look like an owl. They pinched the bridge of my nose so bad I would always take them off and hide them in my backpack. My backpack was one of those clear plastic ones that were popular for like, five minutes. You could see everything I had in there, which was mostly just crumpled up notes and old gum wrappers. I chewed so much gum back then, mostly that bubblegum tape that comes in the little plastic dispenser. \n\nBut no, I don't remember specific days or times I noticed the money was missing. I just know it's gone.\n\nDoctor: I understand you're completely certain about what's happening with your money right now. But let me ask you this differently—is it even possible, in theory, that you could be wrong about this? Not that you think you are wrong, but that it would be possible for you to be mistaken?\n\nPatient: No, it's not possible. I'm not wrong. I know exactly what they're doing. I mean, I came in here because my family and my friends keep saying all these bank papers prove nothing was taken, but they're lying. For over a year I've known they're hiding my money. They use all these things to cover it up—fake bills, fundraisers, trip plans. It's so obvious. \n\nI actually went on a trip once, when I was in middle school, to Washington D.C. It was for a class trip. We rode on this huge charter bus for hours and hours. The seats were covered in this scratchy blue fabric with weird yellow squiggles on it. It looked like someone threw up mustard on a carpet. The girl sitting next to me actually did throw up, right in the aisle. She ate too many of those powdered donuts they sold at the rest stop. I hate powdered donuts. The sugar gets all over your fingers and your clothes, and it looks like you have dandruff. Dandruff is so embarrassing. My brother used to have it really bad, and he would use this special shampoo that smelled like tar. It was a dark brown color and it stained the shower curtain. Our shower curtain had little frogs on it, sitting on lily pads. I always thought frogs were kind of cute, but they feel weird. Like cold and slimy. I tried to catch one in the creek behind our house once, but I slipped on a mossy rock and scraped my knee up so bad. It bled through my favorite jeans. They had little daisies embroidered on the pockets. \n\nBut no, I'm not mistaken about the money. I just have to keep confronting them until they finally tell the truth.\n\nDoctor: You've told me you're certain your family is hiding your money, but when I ask you for the specific details—like exactly how they take it out or where it goes—you say you don't know those things. Can you help me understand: do you think you just haven't figured out all the details yet, or do you actually not believe those details matter because you're so sure about the main thing they're doing?\n\nPatient: I mean... the details don't really matter. I don't need to know exactly how they do it, because I know they're doing it. Like I said, I came in here because my family and my friends keep telling me I'm crazy, saying all these bank papers prove nothing was taken. But I know for over a year they've been hiding my money. They use all those things to cover it up—the bills, the fundraisers, the trip plans. It's all a cover. So I just have to keep confronting them until they finally admit the truth. \n\nConfronting people is really hard, though. I used to hate doing it. I had this roommate once, back when I lived in this tiny apartment above a bakery. The whole place always smelled like yeast and cinnamon, especially at like four in the morning when they started baking. It was nice at first, but then it just made me hungry all the time. I ate so many cinnamon rolls that year. They were huge, like the size of my head, and covered in this thick white frosting that would get all over my nose when I tried to take a bite. Frosting is weird, sometimes it's too sweet and it makes your teeth hurt. My roommate used to buy these little tubs of frosting from the grocery store and just eat it with a spoon while she watched TV. She watched those old soap operas, the ones where everyone is always coming back from the dead or having evil twins. I never really understood the appeal of evil twins. It seems like a lot of work to be evil all the time. \n\nBut yeah, I don't need to figure out every little detail about the bank stuff. I just know what they're doing, and they're lying with those papers, and I won't stop until they tell the truth.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I mean... I don't really know if it fits together like a story. It's just what's happening. I don't need to explain it like a story because it's just the truth. They're hiding my money, and that's it. 

I used to love reading stories, though. When I was little, I had this big book of fairy tales. The cover was dark red, like a cherry, and it had gold letters on it that were all peeling off. I used to pick at the gold flakes when I was bored. I picked at a lot of things when I was bored. Scabs, mostly. I always had scabs on my knees from falling off my bike. My bike was bright pink with a white basket on the front, and it had those sparkly streamers on the handlebars. I thought I was so cool riding it down the street. The wind would blow through the streamers and make this swishing sound. Swishing sounds always remind me of the ocean. I went to the beach once, but the sand got everywhere. It was in my shoes, in my hair, even in my sandwich. Sand in a peanut butter and jelly sandwich is the absolute worst. It crunches when you chew it, and it makes your teeth feel gritty. 

So yeah, I don't know if it's a connected story or whatever. It doesn't have to be. I just know what they're doing.

I'm Dr. [Name], a psychiatrist. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well, I came in because... well, my family and friends keep saying the bank papers prove nothing was taken. But I know. But I won't stop until they admit it.\n\nDoctor: When you think about what your family is doing with your money, do you ever have any doubt about whether it's really happening, or are you completely certain that this is what's going on?\n\nPatient: Doubt? No. No, I don't have any doubt at all. Like I was just saying, I am completely certain. They try to confuse me, you know? They keep waving those bank papers in my face, saying it proves nothing was taken. But I've known for over a year now. I know they're hiding my money and just using all those bills and trip plans to cover it up. \n\nLike that Florida trip they keep talking about... Florida is just way too hot anyway. I went down there when I was little and got a sunburn so bad my shoulders peeled for a whole week. We stayed at this cheap motel by the highway that had a pool, but the water was this weird bright green color. I didn't even want to go in. And there was this little boy who just kept doing cannonballs and splashing water all over my dry towel on the chair. I really hate when my towel gets wet before I even use it, it feels so gross when you try to dry off. \n\nBut... yeah, I have to keep confronting them. They think they can just hide what they took with all their fake fundraisers and vacations, but I'm not dumb. I'm going to keep getting in their faces until they finally tell the truth.
